\subsection{Rank-Powered Translation-Conditioned Protein Mediation for $B^{*}_{Q6}$}
\label{subsec:q6-rank-powered-translation-conditioned-protein-mediation-cross-layer}

\paragraph{Finite object.}
This record tests whether the finite $B^{*}_{Q6}$ residual surface can be read as a measured cross-layer relation from synonymous coding-sequence coordinates to protein abundance through a translation-efficiency readout.  The carrier is the finite matrix surface with rows indexed by nine residual coordinates,
$K_{AAA}$, $Arg_{AGR}$, $Ile_{AUA}$, $Leu_{CUN\_vs\_UUR}$, $Leu_{UUA\_vs\_UUG}$, $Ser_{UCR\_vs\_AGY}$, $Ser_{UCA\_vs\_UCG}$, $Thr_{ACR\_vs\_ACY}$, and $f3_{stress}$, and with columns supplied by matched translation and protein-abundance readouts after residualization.  The residualization controls are the intercept, $\log(cds\_len\_nt)$, the twenty amino-acid composition proportions $A,C,D,E,F,G,H,I,K,L,M,N,P,Q,R,S,T,V,W,Y$, $GC3$, and an $M$-density baseline defined on the union support of the nine projected $B^{*}_{Q6}$ vectors.  In every powered measured-mediation row, the control matrix has $24$ columns and rank $23$, while the residualized $Q$ block has rank $9$, the measured translation block has rank $1$, and the joint $(Q,T)$ block has rank $10$ at tolerance $10^{-10}$.

\paragraph{Measured mediation rows.}
Let $X_{Q}$ denote the residualized nine-column $B^{*}_{Q6}$ coordinate block, let $T$ denote $\log_{10}(TE)$ from measured ribosome profiling with $TE=RPF/mRNA$, and let $Y$ denote $\log_{10}$ protein abundance in parts per million from the matched proteomics table.  The measured decomposition compares the direct $X_Q \to Y$ energy against the remaining direct energy after $T$ is conditioned on, and records the bounded scalar $M^{QTP}_{meas}$ as a descriptive fraction rather than as an intervention effect:
$$
\begin{aligned}
0 \leq M^{QTP}_{meas} \leq 1,\qquad
S^{QT}_{meas} > 0,\qquad
S^{QP} > 0.
\end{aligned}
$$
The powered rows are finite and nondegenerate.  Danio rerio has $13{,}140$ joined CDS rows for the three-way codon, measured-TE, and abundance join, with residual degrees of freedom $13{,}117$; it gives $S^{QT}_{meas}=0.04034217614654355$, $S^{QP}=0.04926578454614652$, raw $M^{QTP}_{meas}=-0.04801486048721136$, and clipped measured fraction $0.0$.  Escherichia coli K-12 MG1655 has $3{,}415$ joined rows and residual degrees of freedom $3{,}392$; it gives $S^{QT}_{meas}=0.009394266754822262$, $S^{QP}=0.10941535951828074$, and $M^{QTP}_{meas}=0.04091158340950673$.  Homo sapiens has $11{,}554$ joined rows and residual degrees of freedom $11{,}531$; it gives $S^{QT}_{meas}=0.018837911344127926$, $S^{QP}=0.04288464996165745$, and $M^{QTP}_{meas}=0.0027966485036786744$.  Saccharomyces cerevisiae has $5{,}128$ joined rows and residual degrees of freedom $5{,}105$; it gives $S^{QT}_{meas}=0.13681931343730436$, $S^{QP}=0.3146818503437042$, and $M^{QTP}_{meas}=0.4793069656345167$.

\paragraph{Energy readback.}
The same rows give the scale of the conditioning step.  In Danio rerio, $X_Q$ explains $1092.0114819316218$ units of residual protein-abundance energy, $T$ explains $16.418999670692678$, $X_Q$ explains $144.76301201119642$ units of residual translation energy, and the remaining direct improvement of $X_Q$ after $T$ is $1144.4442608870015$.  In Escherichia coli K-12 MG1655, the corresponding values are $393.78692134854015$, $35.56347808682994$, $8.637891149939946$, and $377.67647487021645$.  In Homo sapiens, they are $565.3850203147235$, $2.0678517436514716$, $38.66659710161269$, and $563.803837143658$.  In Saccharomyces cerevisiae, they are $675.3275481062249$, $593.5847863333478$, $103.10404057290246$, and $351.63835021403213$.  Thus the yeast row is the strong positive measured-mediation case: it has the largest measured translation survival, the largest protein-abundance survival, and the largest measured bounded mediation fraction.  The Danio row instead records a suppressive or nonabsorbing measured-TE condition, since the raw fraction is negative and the admissible reported fraction is $0$.

\paragraph{Four-case interpretation.}
Each finite row is interpreted by the four-case readback after the shared controls.  Case A is positive $S^{QT}$ and positive $S^{QP}$ with a positive measured absorption fraction; the yeast row is the clearest instance, and the E. coli and human rows are weaker positive instances.  Case B is positive translation survival with direct protein survival but no positive measured absorption; the Danio row falls here.  Case C would be a protein-omics association without translation-readout support, and is not the stated mechanism-facing case.  Case D would be rank-degenerate or all-null survival, and is not counted as evidence.  The rank data exclude the insufficient-organism-count boundary: the rows are not all-$1.0$ artifacts, and the reported ranks $rank(X_Q)=9$, $rank(T)=1$, and $rank(X_Q,T)=10$ show that the measured translation column is not swallowed by the residual coordinate block.

\paragraph{Localization check on the nontranslation remainder.}
A separate finite audit tests whether coarse subcellular localization carries part of the residual abundance component left outside a modeled translation complement.  This audit uses twelve multi-label UniProtKB localization columns and a degree-of-freedom-matched Gaussian null with $200$ trials per organism.  Danio rerio has $1{,}984$ joined rows, $2{,}821$ localization records, $K=12$, and localization absorption $0.009508864544725801$ against null mean $0.0062549946178242525$ and null $95$th percentile $0.011321328712802819$, so it does not exceed the stated null threshold.  Homo sapiens has $16{,}008$ joined rows, $16{,}406$ localization records, $K=12$, and absorption $0.009254913329269913$ against null mean $0.0008032852113983743$ and null $95$th percentile $0.0013758521256775384$, so the localization columns carry excess residual signal in this descriptive projection.  Saccharomyces cerevisiae has $4{,}656$ joined rows, $4{,}831$ localization records, $K=12$, and absorption $0.011225105608516642$ against null mean $0.002689902676583632$ and null $95$th percentile $0.004958116973149795$, again above the finite null threshold.  These localization rows classify part of the remaining abundance signal, but they do not convert it into a causal explanation.

\paragraph{Boundary of the claim.}
The admissible conclusion is narrow.  The finite rows support a rank-powered, cross-sectional statistical relation in which $B^{*}_{Q6}$ residual coordinates survive both measured translation and protein-abundance readbacks, with measured translation absorbing a positive portion of the direct protein-abundance signal in the strongest yeast row and more weakly in E. coli and human.  They do not establish translation dynamics beyond the measured TE contact, folded structural order, physical admissibility, molecular function, perturbation response, temporal causation, mechanism realization, evolutionary direction, or universality.  A direct $q_i \to P_k$ survival score is therefore not promoted to a mechanistic statement unless the matched translation readout supplies the positive conditioning step $q_i \to T_j \to P_k$, and a localization absorption score remains a descriptive residual projection unless a separate perturbational or mechanistic contact is supplied.

\subsection{q6.translation-mediated.system-phenotype-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested against matched translation readouts before any downstream system phenotype readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} If a rank-powered matched panel supplies B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, translation readouts, mRNA controls, and phenotype labels on the same CDS or organism surface, then any admissible Q6-to-phenotype frontier claim must decompose through translation first: q\_i may enter the system\_phenotype layer only as q\_i -> T\_j -> phenotype\_k, with the direct phenotype association rejected as a boundary artifact whenever the controlled translation leg is absent, rank-degenerate, or erased by baseline ablation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS-by-organism matrix carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, translation readouts, and system phenotype labels on the same readout surface. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient-surviving codon residual q\_i is separated from amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls; translation readout T\_j is explicitly measured or externally supplied before any phenotype readout is tested; phenotype association is admitted only after conditioning on the mandatory translation readout. Readback: per-entry gated relation q\_i -> T\_j -> phenotype\_k with break\_condition, AA-composition control, simpler-baseline ablation, and refusal of phenotype interpretation when the translation leg is absent or rank-degenerate. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.physical-admissibility-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream physical-admissibility readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a physical-admissibility boundary only through a decomposition that first tests translation-readout survival and then asks whether any downstream physical-admissibility association remains or is attenuated after conditioning on the translation readout.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS panel carrying B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates, translation readout surfaces, optional protein-omics readbacks, and a future physical-admissibility readout surface. Distinctions: q\_i is amino-acid-quotient residualized before any readout test; T\_j is a measured or modeled translation readout, not a protein mechanism by itself; A physical-admissibility signal may be interpreted only after q\_i -> T\_j survival or an explicit conditioned boundary test. Readback: conditioned cross-layer table separating q\_i -> T\_j survival, q\_i -> physical-admissibility residual association, and translation-conditioned attenuation. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.post-translational-state-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream post-translational protein-state readout such as PTM, localization, or stability is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a post-translational protein-state boundary only through the gated decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k: first translation-readout survival must be established under amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls, and only then may a matched PTM, localization, or stability readout be tested for mediation rather than direct codon-topology association.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite B*\_Q6 residual-coordinate by translation-readout by post-translational protein-state readout surface over matched CDS panels. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives amino-acid quotient before any protein-state interpretation; translation readout T\_j is measured or modeled separately from protein-state readout P\_k; conditioning on T\_j must reduce any direct q\_i to P\_k association before a translation-mediated protein-state claim is allowed. Readback: per-entry discriminant for q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k with null, translation-only, direct protein-state, and mediated cases reported separately. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-stability-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream protein-stability or degradation-rate readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-stability boundary only through a rank-powered matched panel where q\_i first survives a translation readout T\_j under controls, and any downstream stability or degradation readout is reported as translation-conditioned mediation rather than direct codon-topology mechanism.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite organism-by-CDS panel carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, matched translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and protein-stability readout surface where available. Distinctions: each B*\_Q6 coordinate is first amino-acid-quotient residualized; translation readout T\_j must survive controls before any protein-stability readout is interpreted; protein-stability signal is reported only as translation-conditioned residual association, not folding mechanism or phenotype. Readback: per-entry gated matrix q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-stability readout with AA-composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls. Internal structure: relation; rank; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-localization-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory matched translation readouts before any downstream protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may be proposed as relevant to protein localization only through a gated cross-layer decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> L\_k: first test translation-readout survival with amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls, then attach a matched localization readout before interpreting any q\_i-to-localization relation. With the current contact set, the localization leg remains a needs-data boundary rather than a biological claim.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite CDS-indexed surface whose coordinates are B*\_Q6 synonymous residuals, matched translation readouts, mRNA controls, and a downstream protein-localization readout slot. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives the amino-acid quotient before any higher-layer readout is tested; T\_j translation readout is measured or modeled separately from the downstream protein-localization readout; localization interpretation is refused unless q\_i first survives or is conditioned through a translation-readout boundary. Readback: read back only the gated relation q\_i -> T\_j -> localization\_k, with a null boundary when matched localization contacts are absent. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.n3-rank-degenerate.translation-survival-refusal.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates tested against modeled tAI translation readouts across the current three-organism panel under amino-acid composition, GC3, length, and M-density controls

\paragraph{Current claim.} If the controlled B*\_Q6 to modeled-tAI survival matrix is computed on only the current three organisms, the resulting all-1.0 fit must be classified as rank saturation rather than biological survival; the admissible next step is a rank-powered translation-readout panel, not downstream interpretation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{trna-gene-copy-numbers}. External genome annotation resources report organism-level tRNA-Leu gene-copy counts by anticodon family.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; tRNA-Leu gene-copy spectrum. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite 3-organism B*\_Q6 residual-coordinate by modeled-tAI readout surface with control-residualized row space. Distinctions: rank-degenerate all-1.0 survival output is separated from powered translation-readout survival; B*\_Q6 residual coordinates remain translation-readout candidates rather than accepted survival coordinates at n=3. Readback: reject any S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\} pass call when organism count and residual degrees of freedom make the controlled projection saturated. Internal structure: rank; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\paragraph{Mismatch ledger.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion.scope-review}. Status: aligned. Delta: The tAI or stAI contact supplies an external organism-level codon-adaptation weight readback for the residual-basis packet, while BEDC residual coordinates and S\_Q notation remain internal coordinate memory. This contact does not observe ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or a global biological law.

Refinement pressure: Keep the packet at bounded readout scope: a separate reality contact is required before any ribosome, protein-output, mechanism, structure, physical-admissibility, function, or universal-law claim; codon/window geometry cannot supply that contact.

This normalization is not a biological observation and does not convert BEDC codon/window geometry or tAI/stAI weights into translation realization, structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law.

Refinement pressure: Keep the residual-basis packet on the separated-contact route: use normalized ids for packet shape, then require a matched external dataset and Z-controlled statistic before any survival claim. Higher-layer claims still require their own reality contacts.

\subsection{Translation-conditioned four-case survival discriminant}
\label{sec:q6-translation-conditioned-four-case-survival-discriminant-cross-layer}
\origin{ai}

\paragraph{Finite row carrier.}
This record treats the $B^*_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates as finite columns $q_i$ evaluated on matched coding-sequence rows, translation readouts $T_j$, and protein-omics readouts $P_k$. The carrier is not a symbolic claim about codon meaning by itself. It is the concrete matrix obtained after amino-acid composition, organism identity, GC3, coding length, methionine density, mRNA, and simpler baseline controls have been removed from the tested readouts. The intended readback is therefore a controlled cross-layer classification of each matched cell $(q_i,T_j,P_k)$, not a direct biological mechanism.

For the powered run cited here, the coordinate source is the imported $q$-vector table from the $B^*_{Q6}$ translation-survival computation. The translation readout is per-protein modeled tAI,
$$
\begin{aligned}
T &= \frac{\sum_{c} \operatorname{count}(c)\,\operatorname{tAI}(c)}{\sum_{c} \operatorname{count}(c)},
\end{aligned}
$$
where codon counts are combined with all-tRNA copy counts and dos Reis wobble weights. The protein readout is $\log_{10}(\operatorname{abundance\_ppm})$ from measured PAXdb protein abundance joined to real coding-sequence codon counts. This distinction is essential: the $T$ column is a modeled translation-efficiency proxy, while the $P$ column is a measured abundance contact.

\paragraph{Four cases.}
For every residual coordinate and matched pair of readouts, the classification uses the signs of the controlled survival scores $S^{QT}$ and $S^{QP}$, followed by a mediation check through $M^{QTP}$. The local decision rule is
$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{A} &: S^{QT}\leq 0 \;\text{and}\; S^{QP}\leq 0,\\
\mathrm{B} &: S^{QT}>0 \;\text{and}\; S^{QP}\leq 0,\\
\mathrm{C} &: S^{QT}\leq 0 \;\text{and}\; S^{QP}>0,\\
\mathrm{D} &: S^{QT}>0 \;\text{and}\; S^{QP}>0 \;\text{with the direct } q_i\to P_k \text{ term reduced after conditioning on } T_j.
\end{aligned}
$$
Case A means that the controlled data compress the residual coordinate away for both tested layers. Case B means that the coordinate survives at the modeled-translation layer without detected propagation to the measured protein-abundance layer. Case C means that the coordinate predicts protein abundance without mediation by the measured translation readout; it is not a translation explanation. Case D means that the coordinate survives both tests and the protein association is reduced after translation conditioning, which is the local signature of translation-mediated propagation in this finite matrix.

\paragraph{Rank-powered contact.}
The run used a classification threshold of $10^{-12}$ and required rank sufficiency before any positive classification. For \textit{Escherichia coli} K-12 MG1655, the joined table contained $3739$ coding-sequence/protein rows. After the $24$ control columns were residualized, the control rank was $23$, the residual degrees of freedom were $3716$, and the residualized $B^*_{Q6}$ coordinate rank was $9$ against both $T$ and $P$. The residual translation energy was $0.4728468725814029$, the residual protein-abundance energy was $4020.333671802061$, the coordinate energy explained on $T$ was $0.256128714706293$, and the coordinate energy explained on $P$ was $432.89050065533684$. The aggregate controlled fits were
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{QT} &= 0.5416736993691318,\\
R^2_{QP} &= 0.10767526677985896.
\end{aligned}
$$
The coordinate-level classification was complete: $0$ coordinates in case A, $0$ in case B, $0$ in case C, $7$ in case D, and $2$ positive-positive coordinates without the required mediation drop. No coordinate was left unclassified.

For \textit{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, the joined table contained $5815$ coding-sequence/protein rows. After the same $24$ control columns were residualized, the control rank was $23$, the residual degrees of freedom were $5792$, and the residualized coordinate rank was again $9$ against both $T$ and $P$. The residual translation energy was $6.90537888244409$, the residual protein-abundance energy was $2958.1865890925337$, the coordinate energy explained on $T$ was $5.671703404506426$, and the coordinate energy explained on $P$ was $882.6526210803958$. The aggregate controlled fits were
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{QT} &= 0.8213457220900503,\\
R^2_{QP} &= 0.29837624994140827.
\end{aligned}
$$
The classification was also complete in this organism: $0$ coordinates in case A, $0$ in case B, $0$ in case C, $9$ in case D, and $0$ positive-positive coordinates lacking the mediation drop. No coordinate was unclassified.

\paragraph{Readback.}
The finite observation is therefore not merely that some $Q6$ residual coordinate correlates with a protein-abundance column. The observation is that, after the stated controls and rank checks, both organisms retain full residual coordinate rank and place their classified coordinates in the positive translation-positive protein regime, with mediation-supported case D accounting for $7$ of $9$ coordinates in \textit{E. coli} and $9$ of $9$ coordinates in yeast. The two remaining \textit{E. coli} positive-positive coordinates are explicitly held outside case D because the mediation drop condition was not met.

This supports a bounded cross-layer discriminant: $B^*_{Q6}$ residual geometry can be tested against a modeled translation readout and a measured protein-omics readout, and the result separates translation-only survival, protein prediction without measured translation mediation, and translation-conditioned propagation. The present run supplies a powered finite matrix for the two listed organisms; it does not promote the result to a population law over all organisms.

\paragraph{Local boundary.}
No translation dynamics, folding pathway, physical admissibility, molecular function, phenotype, causal mechanism, or universal biological rule follows from the residual coordinates alone. Modeled tAI is not ribosome profiling. Cross-sectional abundance is not a perturbation experiment and is not isolated from mRNA abundance, turnover, localization, post-translational modification, folding, or pathway context. A direct $Q\to P$ association without the mandatory $T$ conditioning step is not admissible as a protein-function or mechanism claim. Case C, when it occurs, must be read only as protein-omics prediction not mediated by the measured translation readout; it may reflect unmeasured translation variables, transcriptional effects, selection, confounding, or non-translation paths. If future matched panels lose residual rank, collapse to case A, or lack matched translation, proteomics, and mRNA contacts, the discriminant becomes a null boundary result rather than evidence for downstream structure, function, physical admissibility, phenotype, or universality.

\subsection{q6.rank-admissible.translation-survival-gate.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates across a rank-audited multi-organism panel with codon usage, tRNA gene-copy or tAI/stAI translation readouts, and controls for amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density

\paragraph{Current claim.} Before any B*\_Q6 coordinate is promoted from codon-topology residual to translation-realization survival, each proposed S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\} entry must pass a rank-admissibility gate: after residualizing q\_i and translation readout T\_j against Z, the remaining design must have non-saturated rank and enough independent organism/readout support to make held-out survival falsifiable. If the gate fails, the entry remains coupling-only or needs\_data even when the fitted in-sample score is positive or equal to 1.0.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{trna-gene-copy-numbers}. External genome annotation resources report organism-level tRNA-Leu gene-copy counts by anticodon family.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; tRNA-Leu gene-copy spectrum. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite residualized design matrix over B*\_Q6 coordinates, organism-indexed translation readout surfaces, and control columns Z. Distinctions: rank-admissible translation-survival entry versus rank-saturated projection artifact; coupling-only contact versus controlled survival contact; translation-readout boundary before any protein, structure, function, or phenotype interpretation. Readback: per-entry admissibility certificate for S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\}: residualized rank, degrees of freedom after Z, held-out translation readout boundary, and break condition for rejecting survival when rank is saturated. Internal structure: rank; relation; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

\paragraph{Cannot-claim boundary.} Do not report S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}>0 from a residualized design whose post-control row space is rank-saturated or has insufficient degrees of freedom.; Do not treat A\textasciicircum{}\{QT\} coupling, modeled tAI contact, or all-1.0 fitted R2 as translation survival.; Do not infer ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, structural order, function realization, or phenotype from this gate without a separate matched translation-readout survival step and downstream contacts.

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-interaction-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through matched translation readouts and protein-abundance controls before any downstream protein-interaction or complex-membership readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-interaction or complex-membership readout only through a gated decomposition that first demonstrates rank-powered translation-readout survival and then tests whether conditioning on matched translation readout and protein abundance removes the direct q\_i association with the interaction readout.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite panel of coding sequences indexed by organism, B*\_Q6 residual coordinate, mandatory translation readout, protein-abundance control, and downstream protein-interaction readout surface. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives amino-acid quotient control or is compressed away; translation readout T\_j mediates or fails to mediate the q\_i to protein-interaction readout association; protein-interaction readout is interpreted only after translation-readout conditioning. Readback: per-entry gated decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-interaction readout with AA composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, mRNA, and protein-abundance controls. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{Q6 Ribosome-Profiling Translation-Efficiency Contact}
\label{subsec:q6-ribosome-te-survival-with-mrna-control-cross-layer}

\paragraph{Finite carrier.}
This record concerns the finite table obtained by crossing nine $B^*_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates with organism-matched ribosome-profiling translation-efficiency readouts.  The coordinate side is the supplied residual list
$K_{AAA}$, $Arg_{AGR}$, $Ile_{AUA}$, $Leu_{CUN\,vs\,UUR}$, $Leu_{UUA\,vs\,UUG}$, $Ser_{UCR\,vs\,AGY}$, $Ser_{UCA\,vs\,UCG}$, $Thr_{ACR\,vs\,ACY}$, and $f3_{stress}$.
The readout side is $\log_{10}(TE)$, with $TE$ defined in the local source rows as ribosome footprint divided by matched mRNA abundance.  Thus the measured response is already normalized by a same-condition transcript contact, and the regression still carries explicit controls for amino-acid composition, organism identity where relevant, GC3, coding-sequence length, and M-density.  The finite question is not whether Q6 geometry is biologically important in general, but whether this residual coordinate block retains positive controlled explanatory energy for measured ribosome-profiling TE after those controls have been removed.

\paragraph{Rank and row audit.}
All four organism panels are powered in the finite-rank sense recorded here: the residualized Q block has rank $9$ in each organism, with $24$ control columns and control rank $23$ at tolerance $10^{-10}$.  The degenerate-fit cutoff is $0.999999$, so all-1.0 or rank-saturated fits would be rejected rather than promoted.  The row counts and join rates are organism-specific.  In \emph{Danio rerio}, $14410$ CDS rows are joined, $13140$ genes are used, and the ribosome-TE join hit rate is $0.9118667591950035$; after controls the residual degrees of freedom are $13117$.  In \emph{Escherichia coli} K12 MG1655, $3739$ CDS rows are joined, $3415$ genes are used, the hit rate is $0.9133458143888741$, and the residual degrees of freedom are $3392$.  In \emph{Homo sapiens}, $19053$ CDS rows are joined, $11554$ genes are used, the hit rate is $0.6064136881331024$, and the residual degrees of freedom are $11531$.  In \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, $5815$ CDS rows are joined, $5128$ genes are used, the hit rate is $0.8818572656921754$, and the residual degrees of freedom are $5105$.

\paragraph{Controlled survival values.}
The supplied powered run reports positive measured-TE residual survival in all four organism panels, using positivity threshold $10^{-12}$ for partial $R^2$.  The measured $R^2_{QT}$ values are $0.04034217614654355$ for \emph{Danio rerio}, $0.009394266754822262$ for \emph{Escherichia coli} K12 MG1655, $0.018837911344127926$ for \emph{Homo sapiens}, and $0.13681931343730436$ for \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}.  The surviving-coordinate count is $9$ in each case, matching the full residual-coordinate count.  The corresponding residual-response energies after the control projection are $3588.378859021949$, $919.4854026798789$, $2052.594706241978$, and $753.5781168800304$, respectively; the Q-block explained energies are $144.76301201119642$, $8.637891149939946$, $38.66659710161269$, and $103.10404057290246$.  Equivalently, the finite calculation has the following organism-level form:
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{QT}(\textit{D. rerio}) &= 0.04034217614654355,\\
R^2_{QT}(\textit{E. coli K12}) &= 0.009394266754822262,\\
R^2_{QT}(\textit{H. sapiens}) &= 0.018837911344127926,\\
R^2_{QT}(\textit{S. cerevisiae}) &= 0.13681931343730436.
\end{aligned}
$$
These values support the limited survival statement: the nine-coordinate Q6 residual block is not compressed away by the stated controls in this measured TE contact.

\paragraph{Boundary of interpretation.}
The conclusion is deliberately narrow.  Positive controlled association with measured TE does not by itself establish translation mechanism, elongation speed, mature protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, organism-independent directionality, phenotype, or biological function.  It also does not identify a causal role for any individual coordinate.  The readout is condition-specific, cross-sectional, and drawn from one or a small number of ribosome-profiling studies per organism.  Agreement or disagreement with modeled tAI-style quantities would be only a coordinate-pattern comparison unless a separate contact supplies the relevant measurement.  If a future panel has no positive controlled $S^{QT}$ entries, loses Q-rank after control residualization, collapses into a degenerate fit, or contains too few nonredundant rows, this record should be read as needs-data or null for that contact rather than as survival.

\paragraph{Finite verdict.}
For the supplied finite rows, the Q6 residual coordinate block passes the translation-efficiency contact at the association level.  The promotion is only from codon-topology residual to measured ribosome-profiling TE association under matched mRNA and sequence-composition controls.  No translation realization, folding realization, physical admissibility, function claim, mechanistic claim, or universal biological law follows from this chapter unless another finite contact is added at that layer.

\origin{ai}

\subsection{Translation-First Three-Matrix Readout for the $B^*_{Q6}$ Residual Panel}
\label{sec:q6-three-matrix-translation-first-protein-readout-cross-layer}

\paragraph{Finite carrier.}
This chapter records a cross-layer BioReality claim for a finite panel of coding sequences indexed by organism, a $B^*_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinate, a translation readout, and an optional downstream protein-abundance readout. The carrier is not an amino-acid sequence claim. It is a residual-coordinate claim after the amino-acid quotient: the entries distinguish a synonymous coordinate that survives amino-acid composition from the amino-acid sequence component itself. The nine residual coordinates used in the computed panel are
$$
\begin{aligned}
&K\_AAA,\quad Arg\_AGR,\quad Ile\_AUA,\quad Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR,\quad Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG,\\
&Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY,\quad Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG,\quad Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY,\quad f3\_stress.
\end{aligned}
$$
The control block $Z$ consists of an intercept, $\log(\mathrm{cds\_len\_nt})$, the twenty amino-acid composition proportions $A,C,D,E,F,G,H,I,K,L,M,N,P,Q,R,S,T,V,W,Y$, $GC3$ computed from real coding-sequence codon counts, and an $M$-density baseline equal to the fraction of sense codons on the union support of the nine projected $B^*_{Q6}$ vectors. Thus the finite row space tests whether a synonymous residual coordinate contributes after the main sequence-composition explanations have already been removed.

\paragraph{Ordered matrix semantics.}
The readout boundary is ordered. First, for a residual coordinate $q_i$ and a translation readout $T_j$, the survival entry $S^{QT}_{ij}$ must be estimated under the full $Z$ controls. This object is not the same as a modeled codon-adaptation coupling $A^{QT}_{ij}$: $A^{QT}_{ij}$ can record a rank-degenerate association, whereas $S^{QT}_{ij}$ is a controlled survival score in the finite panel. Only after a positive and rank-powered $S^{QT}_{ij}$ has been obtained may a protein readout $P_k$ enter the record. At that stage the direct protein entry $S^{QP}_{ik}$ and the translation-mediated entry $M^{QTP}_{ijk}$ are separated by conditioning on the matched translation readout. The local admissibility rule is therefore
$$
\begin{aligned}
q_i \leadsto P_k\quad \hbox{is interpretable only through}\quad
S^{QT}_{ij}\ \hbox{first, then}\ S^{QP}_{ik}\ \hbox{and}\ M^{QTP}_{ijk}.
\end{aligned}
$$
If all controlled $S^{QT}_{ij}$ entries are nonpositive, the $B^*_{Q6}$ basis remains a code-read or codon-usage residual basis, and no downstream protein-readout mediation statement is admitted.

\paragraph{Rows and ranks.}
The finite computation joined four organism panels. For \emph{Danio rerio}, the all-readout join contains $n=13140$ rows, with $14410$ coding sequences reported in the abundance join, $14410$ valid abundance records by protein, $13140$ valid mRNA records by protein, $24$ control columns, and $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$. For \emph{Escherichia coli} K-12 MG1655, the corresponding numbers are $n=3415$, $3739$, $3747$, $3415$, $24$, and $23$. For \emph{Homo sapiens}, they are $n=11554$, $19053$, $19483$, $11554$, $24$, and $23$. For \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, they are $n=5128$, $5815$, $6351$, $5128$, $24$, and $23$. These ranks are part of the claim: an all-$1.0$ residual fit on a tiny saturated row space is not treated as survival. The computation is meaningful here because the residual panel is tested against thousands of joined rows while the control design has rank $23$.

\paragraph{Modeled complement and mRNA absorption.}
The existing finite calculation is a downstream descriptive projection, not the translation-survival matrix itself. It projects the modeled-tAI complement abundance residual $\widehat P_{Q\perp Tmod}$ onto residualized $\log_{10}$ mRNA. The squared norms and modeled complements are organism-specific:
$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{Danio\ rerio}:&\quad \|P_Q\|^2=1092.0114815505467,\quad \|P_{Q\perp Tmod}\|^2=919.9445878158525,\quad d=0.8424312412078521,\\
\mathrm{E.\ coli\ K12}:&\quad \|P_Q\|^2=393.78692095732885,\quad \|P_{Q\perp Tmod}\|^2=261.0102870984463,\quad d=0.6628211177352172,\\
\mathrm{Homo\ sapiens}:&\quad \|P_Q\|^2=565.3850201235074,\quad \|P_{Q\perp Tmod}\|^2=484.31956581711796,\quad d=0.8566190269974241,\\
\mathrm{S.\ cerevisiae}:&\quad \|P_Q\|^2=675.3275479192903,\quad \|P_{Q\perp Tmod}\|^2=151.18264752811882,\quad d=0.22386566043976477.
\end{aligned}
$$
The same run reports small mRNA absorption fractions: $0.011973033879985792$ for \emph{Danio rerio}, $0.0017706954554908886$ for \emph{E. coli} K-12, $0.007858222847072218$ for \emph{Homo sapiens}, and $0.0018976054721605224$ for \emph{S. cerevisiae}. The combined descriptive quantities are respectively $0.8322280386743373$, $0.6615987644523418$, $0.8498079619223776$, and $0.223360891719316$. These numbers say that the mRNA projection has been computed and bounded in the joined panels; they do not identify a causal transcript mechanism.

\paragraph{Join sensitivity.}
The modeled complement was also compared against the abundance-only complement experiment. The absolute differences are $0.03219747984141397$ for \emph{Danio rerio}, $0.00017488612263594217$ for \emph{E. coli} K-12, $0.06427590857052146$ for \emph{Homo sapiens}, and $0.012133464712697745$ for \emph{S. cerevisiae}. The $0.02$ match criterion is met for \emph{E. coli} K-12 and \emph{S. cerevisiae}, but not for \emph{Danio rerio} or \emph{Homo sapiens}. The failure cases are informative because mRNA availability changes the protein-identifier join used by the projection. They are not negative biological laws; they mark where the finite panel changes when the transcript contact is imposed.

\paragraph{Local boundary.}
The claim is deliberately narrow. Codon-usage tables provide per-organism sequence composition. tAI or stAI weights provide modeled organism-scoped codon-adaptation weights. Ribosome profiling and matched mRNA abundance can supply a translation-efficiency readout when identifier matching and normalization are under control. Proteomics contacts can supply steady-state protein abundance. None of these contacts, alone or in the present descriptive projection, licenses translation realization, folding, physical admissibility, protein function, mechanism, phenotype, or universality. A positive protein-abundance association is not promoted unless a separate matched translation readout first yields a controlled $S^{QT}$ entry and the subsequent $q_i\to P_k$ signal is partitioned into translation-mediated and non-mediated components. The present finite record therefore supports a translation-first protocol for testing $B^*_{Q6}$ cross-layer readouts, while leaving the central survival matrix $S^{QT}$ as the required gate for any stronger biological interpretation.

\subsection{q6.translation-mediated.protein-localization-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through matched translation readouts before any downstream protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-localization boundary only if a matched translation readout is first attached and survives controls; without positive S\textasciicircum{}QT and mediation through T\_j, any protein-localization association remains a bounded protein-omics readback rather than a DNA-to-localization mechanism.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matrix of B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates crossed with matched translation-efficiency readouts and protein-localization readout surfaces over externally supplied CDS panels. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient residual coordinate q\_i versus controlled CDS baseline Z; mandatory translation readout T\_j before any protein-localization interpretation; protein-localization readout P\_k treated only as downstream bounded readback, not as mechanism by itself; mediation contrast between direct q\_i -> P\_k association and q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k absorption. Readback: per-entry gated readback of S\textasciicircum{}QT(q\_i,T\_j), S\textasciicircum{}QP(q\_i,P\_k), and M\textasciicircum{}QTP(q\_i,T\_j,P\_k) with AA-composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-localization.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory matched translation readouts before any protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may be proposed as a cross-layer localization-boundary candidate only if it first survives a rank-powered translation-readout test and then shows a bounded same-row relation to matched protein-localization readout under organism, amino-acid composition, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-protein-localization-readout}. per-protein localization, compartment, fractionation, imaging, or proximity-labeling readouts for externally measured or curated proteins

Can test: protein-localization readout after organism and protein/CDS identifier matching; cross\_layer\_relation boundary only after a separate same-row translation readout and matched controls are attached. Cannot test: translation realization from localization rows alone; protein folding; structural order; physical admissibility; function realization; system phenotype; protein trafficking mechanism; compartment identity mechanism; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS-by-organism panel carrying B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates, same-row translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and protein-localization readback labels. Distinctions: AA-quotient-surviving codon residual coordinate q\_i versus amino-acid sequence baseline; translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}QT(q\_i,T\_j|Z\_T) before any protein-localization interpretation; localization association only after conditioning on translation readout T\_j and mRNA controls. Readback: per-entry discriminant for q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-localization readout: compressed, translation-only, localization-predictive without measured translation mediation, or translation-mediated localization boundary. Internal structure: coordinate; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.
