\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-localization-readout.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through same-row matched translation readouts before any matched protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} For a rank-powered matched CDS panel, each B*\_Q6 coordinate may be tested against protein-localization readouts only after the mandatory translation-readout boundary is attached; admissible outcomes distinguish compression, translation-only survival, localization association not mediated by measured translation, and translation-conditioned localization mediation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-protein-localization-readout}. per-protein localization, compartment, fractionation, imaging, or proximity-labeling readouts for externally measured or curated proteins

Can test: protein-localization readout after organism and protein/CDS identifier matching; cross\_layer\_relation boundary only after a separate same-row translation readout and matched controls are attached. Cannot test: translation realization from localization rows alone; protein folding; structural order; physical admissibility; function realization; system phenotype; protein trafficking mechanism; compartment identity mechanism; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite same-row CDS surface carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, a matched translation readout, mRNA controls, and a matched protein-localization label/readout. Distinctions: q\_i is first residualized against amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls; translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\} must be non-vacuous before localization readback is interpreted; localization readout is treated only as a bounded protein-state surface, not as function or phenotype. Readback: per-entry relation q\_i -> T\_j -> L\_k where T\_j is a translation readout and L\_k is a matched protein-localization readout, with null status if the translation step is absent or rank-degenerate. Internal structure: relation; closure; rank.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-mediated.protein-localization.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through matched translation readouts before any matched protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} For a rank-powered matched panel, each B*\_Q6 coordinate may be tested against protein-localization readouts only after the coordinate first passes a nondegenerate translation-readout survival test; any remaining localization signal must be decomposed into translation-mediated and non-translation residual components.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-protein-localization-readout}. per-protein localization, compartment, fractionation, imaging, or proximity-labeling readouts for externally measured or curated proteins

Can test: protein-localization readout after organism and protein/CDS identifier matching; cross\_layer\_relation boundary only after a separate same-row translation readout and matched controls are attached. Cannot test: translation realization from localization rows alone; protein folding; structural order; physical admissibility; function realization; system phenotype; protein trafficking mechanism; compartment identity mechanism; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite same-row CDS surface carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, matched translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched protein-localization readout labels or scores. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient residual coordinate q\_i is separated from amino-acid sequence and baseline codon composition; translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}QT\_i must be evaluated before any localization readout is interpreted; protein-localization readout is treated as a bounded downstream protein-omics surface, not as function or phenotype. Readback: read back only the cross-layer relation Q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-localization readout under AA-composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls. Internal structure: coordinate; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\subsection{q6.four-case.translation-protein-discriminant.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences decomposed across matched translation readouts and matched protein abundance readouts into the A/B/C/D translation-protein discriminant cases

\paragraph{Current claim.} For a rank-powered matched panel, each B*\_Q6 residual coordinate should be assigned to exactly one controlled four-case translation-protein readout class by jointly computing S\textasciicircum{}QT, S\textasciicircum{}QP, and the Q->T->P mediation drop; only case D supports a bounded translation-mediated protein-readout relation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite B*\_Q6 residual-coordinate by translation-readout by protein-readout survival tensor over matched organism/CDS rows. Distinctions: case A: S\textasciicircum{}QT <= 0 and S\textasciicircum{}QP <= 0, residual coordinate compresses away on both translation and protein readouts; case B: S\textasciicircum{}QT > 0 and S\textasciicircum{}QP <= 0, translation-level irreducible without propagation to the chosen protein readout; case C: S\textasciicircum{}QT <= 0 and S\textasciicircum{}QP > 0, protein-omics predictive residual not supported as mediated by the measured translation readout; case D: S\textasciicircum{}QT > 0 and S\textasciicircum{}QP > 0 with direct Q-to-P signal reduced after conditioning on T, translation-mediated protein-readout support. Readback: four-case boundary table per (q\_i,T\_j,P\_k) after residualizing by amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, mRNA abundance, and simpler codon-usage baselines. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-localization.boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested against matched translation readouts and matched protein-localization readouts, with mRNA controls, before any structure, function, or phenotype interpretation

\paragraph{Current claim.} If a rank-powered matched panel contains B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, same-row translation readouts, mRNA controls, and protein-localization readouts, then each localization entry may be tested only as a conditioned cross-layer relation Q\_i -> T\_j -> L\_k; positive direct localization predictivity without translation mediation remains a bounded protein-readout association, not a mechanism or phenotype claim.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-protein-localization-readout}. per-protein localization, compartment, fractionation, imaging, or proximity-labeling readouts for externally measured or curated proteins

Can test: protein-localization readout after organism and protein/CDS identifier matching; cross\_layer\_relation boundary only after a separate same-row translation readout and matched controls are attached. Cannot test: translation realization from localization rows alone; protein folding; structural order; physical admissibility; function realization; system phenotype; protein trafficking mechanism; compartment identity mechanism; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite same-row table of B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, matched CDS translation readouts, mRNA controls, and protein-localization readout labels or scores. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient-surviving synonymous residual coordinate versus amino-acid-sequence component; translation-readout-mediated localization association versus direct unmediated codon residual association; bounded localization readout versus structure, function, or phenotype claim. Readback: per-entry conditioned relation Q\_i -> T\_j -> L\_k with reported break condition, amino-acid composition control, mRNA control, organism/GC3/length/M-density controls, and refusal of downstream structure/function/phenotype interpretation. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{mRNA-controlled translation-efficiency contact for the $B^*_{Q6}$ residual surface}\label{subsec:q6-mrna-controlled-translation-efficiency-survival-cross-layer}
\origin{ai}

\paragraph{Finite readout surface.}
This record concerns a finite CDS-by-organism surface on which nine $B^*_{Q6}$ residual coordinates are tested against organism-matched translation-efficiency readouts. The coordinate list is
$K\_AAA$, $Arg\_AGR$, $Ile\_AUA$, $Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR$, $Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG$, $Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY$, $Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG$, $Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY$, and $f3\_stress$. The control design contains an intercept, $\log(\mathrm{cds\_len\_nt})$, the twenty amino-acid composition proportions computed from real CDS codon counts, $GC3$, and an $M$-density baseline equal to the fraction of sense codons on the union support of the nine projected $B^*_{Q6}$ vectors. The translation-efficiency contact is not a raw ribosome-count contact alone: it is accepted only where the ribosome-profiling readout is paired to matched mRNA abundance, so that the translation-efficiency quantity is separated from transcription-layer abundance before the $Q_i$ residual is interpreted as a translation-efficiency survival entry.

\paragraph{Ranked organism rows.}
The measured-TE row set is rank-powered in the supplied panel. For \emph{Danio rerio}, the all-readout join has $n=13140$ CDS rows, $14410$ CDS rows reported before measured-TE restriction, $14410$ valid abundance rows, $13140$ valid TE rows, $24$ control columns, and $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$. For \emph{Escherichia coli} K12 MG1655, the corresponding values are $n=3415$, $3739$ reported CDS rows, $3747$ valid abundance rows, $3415$ valid TE rows, $24$ control columns, and $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$. For \emph{Homo sapiens}, they are $n=11554$, $19053$ reported CDS rows, $19483$ valid abundance rows, $11554$ valid TE rows, $24$ control columns, and $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$. For \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, they are $n=5128$, $5815$ reported CDS rows, $6351$ valid abundance rows, $5128$ valid TE rows, $24$ control columns, and $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$. Thus the finite design distinguishes three outcomes: a $Q_i$ coordinate may remain after the translation-efficiency and mRNA boundary is imposed, it may be compressed away by that boundary, or its apparent signal may be reassigned to transcription-layer abundance rather than translation realization.

\paragraph{Measured-TE absorption scalars.}
The observed projection quantities are small on the measured-TE absorption axis. The absorbed fractions by measured TE are $0.01759429001506974$ for \emph{Danio rerio}, $0.0010982956489726373$ for \emph{E. coli} K12 MG1655, $0.0028947149257963897$ for \emph{Homo sapiens}, and $0.0002762783754375593$ for \emph{S. cerevisiae}. Their combined residual distances are respectively $0.8276090159036916$, $0.6620871850159149$, $0.854133502300174$, and $0.2237928123367028$. The modeled-complement distances on the measured-TE-restricted join are $0.8424312412078521$, $0.6628211177352172$, $0.8566190269974241$, and $0.22386566043976477$. In norm-square terms, the corresponding $(\lVert P_Q\rVert^2,\lVert P_{Q\perp Tmod}\rVert^2)$ pairs are $(1092.0114815505467,919.9445878158525)$, $(393.78692095732885,261.0102870984463)$, $(565.3850201235074,484.31956581711796)$, and $(675.3275479192903,151.18264752811882)$.

$$
\begin{aligned}
S^{QT}_{i,TE} &> 0 \quad \text{means that a residual coordinate survives the controlled TE boundary,}\\
S^{QT}_{i,TE} &\leq 0 \quad \text{means that the coordinate is not retained by this measured contact.}
\end{aligned}
$$

The finite data supplied here report the descriptive projection and absorption quantities; they do not supply per-coordinate $S^{QT}_{i,TE}$ entries, $p$-values, or a declared significance threshold. The chapter therefore records a controlled contact surface and its measured absorption scalars, not a population-level declaration that a named coordinate has crossed a survival threshold.

\paragraph{Join self-checks.}
The measured-TE restriction changes the join in two organisms enough to fail the $0.02$ complement-agreement check. \emph{Danio rerio} changes from the full-abundance complement reference $d=0.874628721049266$ on $14410$ rows to $d=0.8424312412078521$ on $13140$ rows, with absolute difference $0.03219747984141397$. \emph{Homo sapiens} changes from $d=0.9208949355679455$ on $19053$ rows to $d=0.8566190269974241$ on $11554$ rows, with absolute difference $0.06427590857052146$. By contrast, \emph{E. coli} K12 MG1655 changes by only $0.00017488612263594217$, from $0.6629960038578532$ on $3739$ rows to $0.6628211177352172$ on $3415$ rows, and \emph{S. cerevisiae} changes by $0.012133464712697745$, from $0.21173219572706703$ on $5815$ rows to $0.22386566043976477$ on $5128$ rows. These self-checks are part of the readout: in the two failing cases, measured-TE availability itself changes the protein-identifier join enough that the numerical contact must be read as conditionally joined rather than as a full-panel invariant.

\paragraph{tRNA-copy comparison.}
A separate modeled-tAI comparison was computed on three organisms. The tRNA gene-copy tables contain $8846$ parsed tRNA genes and $57$ anticodon classes for \emph{Danio rerio}, $431$ genes and $47$ anticodon classes for \emph{Homo sapiens}, and $273$ genes and $41$ anticodon classes for \emph{S. cerevisiae}. The usable measured-tAI record counts are $8607$, $422$, and $268$, respectively, with zero raw-$W$ geometric-mean fill count in all three cases. The tRNA-copy absorbed fractions are $0.020817102845637092$, $5.426229300865148\times 10^{-5}$, and $0.001530747712165342$, with combined distances $0.835657382343872$, $0.92041342656924$, and $0.20648621019305421$. On the full join, the modeled-complement self-checks exactly match the complement references in all three organisms: $0.874628721049266$ on $14410$ rows for \emph{Danio rerio}, $0.9208949355679455$ on $19053$ rows for \emph{Homo sapiens}, and $0.21173219572706703$ on $5815$ rows for \emph{S. cerevisiae}. This comparison is useful as a bounded translation-proxy contrast, but it is not direct tRNA abundance measurement and does not convert the measured-TE contact into a causal mediator analysis.

\paragraph{Local interpretation.}
The finite result is a cross-layer relation between synonymous residual geometry and measured translation efficiency under explicit controls. The reported numbers support the claim that the panel can compute controlled survival and absorption readbacks for the $B^*_{Q6}$ residual basis. They also show that measured TE absorbs only a small fraction of the modeled-complement abundance residual in the supplied runs, while the organism joins and rank conditions determine which rows are actually being tested. A positive per-coordinate survival entry, when supplied, would identify a coordinate retained by this controlled translation-efficiency interface. If all controlled entries are non-positive, the $B^*_{Q6}$ residual basis remains outside this measured translation-efficiency realization for the present contact.

\paragraph{Boundary of the claim.}
Nothing in this chapter by itself establishes mature protein abundance, folding, structural order, physical admissibility, biological function, molecular mechanism, phenotype, universality, or a global biological law. Ribosome-profiling TE is a condition-specific statistical readout; tRNA gene copy number is a proxy used to build a modeled translation score; and an absorbed fraction is descriptive rather than causal. Any claim at the protein, structure, mechanism, function, phenotype, or universal-law layer requires an independent contact at that layer, with its own rows, controls, and failure conditions.

\subsection{q6.three-organism.rank-degenerate.translation-survival-refusal.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates tested against modeled tAI or stAI translation-readout surfaces in a three-organism panel before rank-powered expansion

\paragraph{Current claim.} For the current three-organism B*\_Q6 panel, residualized modeled-tAI S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\} is a rank-saturated boundary object: it may record coupling and the need for a larger panel, but it cannot support survival until a rank-powered multi-organism translation-readout surface is attached.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite 3-organism B*\_Q6 by modeled-tAI residualized readout matrix surface. Distinctions: rank-degenerate three-organism translation-readout surface versus rank-powered multi-organism translation-survival surface; coupling contact versus survival claim under amino-acid composition, GC3, length, M-density, organism, and baseline controls; nonzero A\textasciicircum{}\{QT\} modeled-tAI coupling versus inadmissible S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\} survival at saturated residual rank. Readback: read back only the rank boundary of S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}: an all-1.0 or saturated fit at n=3 is classified as needs\_data, not translation survival. Internal structure: rank; relation; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-mediated.protein-localization-readout.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested against matched translation readouts and matched protein-localization readouts under amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls

\paragraph{Current claim.} For a rank-powered matched panel, a B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may be proposed as a protein-localization-facing cross-layer distinction only if it first survives against a translation readout T\_j under controls and the Q\_i -> localization association is reduced or explained by conditioning on that translation readout; otherwise the localization contact is a bounded null or non-translation residual boundary.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-protein-localization-readout}. per-protein localization, compartment, fractionation, imaging, or proximity-labeling readouts for externally measured or curated proteins

Can test: protein-localization readout after organism and protein/CDS identifier matching; cross\_layer\_relation boundary only after a separate same-row translation readout and matched controls are attached. Cannot test: translation realization from localization rows alone; protein folding; structural order; physical admissibility; function realization; system phenotype; protein trafficking mechanism; compartment identity mechanism; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite CDS-by-organism rows carrying B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates, same-row translation readout surfaces, matched mRNA controls, and matched protein-localization labels or scores. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient-surviving codon residual q\_i versus amino-acid sequence and baseline controls; translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}QT(q\_i,T\_j) versus rank-saturated or baseline-only fit; localization readout survival only after conditioning on same-row translation readout T\_j and mRNA control; null boundary where localization association without translation survival is not interpreted as translation mechanism. Readback: per-entry gated readback: q\_i may approach protein-localization interpretation only through a nondegenerate translation-readout step S\textasciicircum{}QT>0 and a tested mediation/conditioning relation against localization; otherwise it remains a bounded cross-layer boundary proposal. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout
